#5023. In Jordyn Woods-Towns’s NYC Home, Every Day is Knicksmas: “The Whole House Is Knicks-ed Out Already!”

The most wonderful time of the year holds a special place in Jordyn Woods-Towns’s heart—and no, we’re not talking about the start of basketball season. The fashion designer got engaged to Knicks phenom Karl-Anthony Towns on Christmas Eve 2025, and after a whirlwind 2026 including an NBA championship win for the New York NBA team (possibly courtesy of her viral accessory-turned-amulet) and a clifftop wedding replete with stunning details, the couple can’t wait to create festive memories as husband and wife.

“This is my first married Christmas,” Woods-Town tells AD. “I’ve only been married three months, but it’s our first holiday season, so I definitely need to try to figure out what our new traditions are.”

The newlywed reveals that she created an entire PowerPoint presentation to prep attendees for her bachelorette party, so you’d best believe she appreciates a little organization when it comes to getting in the spirit for big days. Planner that she is, Woods-Towns linked up with design marketplace Minted for her first family Christmas card and a curated suite of picks to welcome one particularly merry and bright yuletide season of life. Below, AD gets her take on holiday colors, basketball-themed decor, and more.

In anticipation of the holiday season, Woods-Towns teamed up with Minted to curate a selection of festive stationery and decor.

Architectural Digest: Have you always been a big holiday person?

Jordyn Woods-Towns: Every single season, I decorate. On Valentine’s Day I have the girls over to do a charcuterie board with salami roses and chocolate covered strawberries. Karl and I went crazy for Halloween; we turned the entire front yard into a whole different space with a graveyard and a 20-foot-tall skeleton. On Christmas, I love a theme.

For a couple years I went with a Candy Cane Lane vibe and now I’m in my chic New York era. I love a Ralph Lauren Christmas. I love the natural linen and natural materials. I’ve been really into natural florals, textural things. I love feathers, incorporating some ostrich feathers throughout—I do a lot of that with my brand, and I feel like feathers are really having a moment right now.

In New York it’s been hard because I don’t drive and I don’t have my car. I can’t just run down to HomeGoods, grab something, and come back!

What’s your decor looking like this year?

I’ve been really into all one shade. There were a lot of greens in our wedding and green is my color of the season.

Woods-Towns is leaning toward florals and green for holiday decorating this year.

The tree’s already green. It could be kind of cool, full green-on-green. Now I’m the Grinch. [laughs]

With your Christmas Eve engagement last year, the holiday is a sweet part of your love story. What sorts of traditions are you looking forward to starting with Karl?

I started with the holiday card with Minted. This is our first holiday card as a family. Obviously after you have kids, it kind of changes, but right now we’re at a time where life is just… he has a [Christmas Day] game and so we’re like, “Okay, we’re living the young, fast life.” But as time goes on, as our marriage goes on, we’re going to build different traditions. We love to be with our family and I always cook every holiday, so I’ll definitely be cooking after the game.

Is there a signature dish you like to serve?

Karl’s a picky eater. He loves chicken, so some version of chicken. He’s never had beef or pork or anything in his whole entire life. It’s just us, so some type of chicken and sides. Pretty simple.

I wanted to use a photo from our wedding that really felt like us, something that not everyone has seen, to tie in our whole year. It was a green marbled border design with gold foil border by Jenna Holcomb. There are those shades of green, since there’s a lot of green incorporated into our wedding. Olive is very timeless, and it felt chic and like us, like something that people could keep. I don’t want to say everyone’s going to want to put a photo of us in their house, but I know our family [will].

The Towns family’s holiday card. The design is “Marbled” by Jenna Holcomb.

Are you hosting bigger parties this season or just keeping it more intimate with the celebrations?

No huge parties, just because I don’t have the capacity to do that right now, but definitely getting the family together. We don’t have a huge family, so it should be pretty intimate, hopefully doing something with the team getting together. I think one of the girls was talking about doing a holiday party—even though I love to be the host, I don’t mind just showing up.

I would say it’s all in the details. I think through everything. But overall, it’s about the vibe that you’re bringing, the energy, and what you’re putting out. People remember the music, the food, and how they felt.

It’s been a busy 2026 for Woods-Towns between then-fiancé Karl-Anthony Towns’s successful championship run with the New York Knicks and her August wedding, but she’s keeping her energy high heading into holiday party season. “I feel like there’s an event every other day. But it’s not going to be like this forever. I had to change my mindset from, ‘Wow, I’m so tired,’ to, ‘Wow, I’m so lucky to be able to do this.’”

Are you planning on sprinkling any Knicks Easter eggs throughout your home decor or in your gifting this year?

There’s so many Knicks things in my house that it’s hard to escape. It’s already everywhere, the whole house is Knicks-ed out already! But I don’t mind Knicks-themed gifting—people love the Knicks.

Karl’s very hard to gift for, and his birthday’s actually right before the holidays. So it’s hard to figure out two really good gifts in a short amount of time. Gift giving is one of my love languages, and I love getting people things that they really can utilize in their everyday life. I need to think about that.

How else do you get in the holiday spirit at home?

I already bought Christmas pajamas and I already watched The Grinch twice with my niece and nephew two days ago. So I’m ready!

#5021. 9 Best Small Sectional Sofas That Are Truly Stylish (2026)

A sprawling living room might be the dream, but having a small space doesn’t preclude you from being able to stretch out on the couch. Sectionals specifically designed for smaller rooms prove that cozy movie nights and afternoon naps aren’t reserved exclusively for those with a massive floor plan. In fact, choosing a sectional with the right proportions stands to make a smaller room feel larger. There’s no reason to settle for a hodgepodge of chairs and ottomans when a thoughtfully selected sectional can transform every seat into the best seat in the house.

Being mindful of a space’s dimensions is always important when shopping for furniture, but especially so with a small room, where every inch may matter. Still, a variety of clever configurations—including L-shaped sectionals designed to nestle into corners, modular designs that are easily rearranged, and sofas with reversible chaises that give you more flexibility—cater to seemingly every layout. Add in performance fabrics, washable slipcovers, and supportive cushions, and you’ve got a recipe for some seriously comfortable yet compact lounging. Ahead, we’re sharing our favorite sectionals for small spaces that ensure every square foot counts.

The best-selling Abisko sectional from Article is made even more beautiful by colorful, performance velvet upholstery. Suitable for those with little ones and pets running around, the fabric is non-absorptive, meaning spills and crumbs won’t stay embedded in its weave. Two orientations—chaise and L-shaped—allow you to Tetris its silhouette into your space as you see fit.

Instead of opting for a giant L-shaped sectional that will take up the entire room, aim for something with a smaller footprint. Available in performance fabric and three leg finishes, this sectional couch is ideal for a tiny living space and a major upgrade from a smaller loveseat thanks to the chaise addition. While not an official fold-out sleeper sofa, the back cushions flop back to give you enough space should you be inclined to nap. Contributor David Kaufman, who tested it out for us, calls it “a great starter sofa for a first home or apartment that you won’t have to send to a landfill when you’re ready to move.”

Similar to the Thuma sectional contributor Evan Russum told us “feels solid and durable,” the Essential Sofa follows the brand’s signature simple-but-considered aesthetic with a minimalist silhouette,rounded cushions, and a grounded wooden base. Raised armrests add sculptural presence without overwhelming a small living room, while the detachable ottoman lets you switch between a left- or right-facing chaise—so everyone can have a turn on their favorite side of the sofa.

Not your average grey couch, AllModern’s Jossa sectional is minimalist in looks but generous on comfort, thanks to its dense foam padding that offers plenty of support without feeling stiff. Easy to de-assemble, the modular design allows the sofa to evolve with your seating needs. Craving two separate lounge chairs? Done. Prefer a love seat? Just slide the two larger seats side-by-side. Today’s layout doesn’t have to be tomorrow’s.

The slim arms and low profile on this this reversible three-piece sectional make the Unwind especially suitable for a limited living area as it doesn’t monopolize the space. Five neutral color variations all come in a linen blend, but olive’s grounding tones are speaking most to us. This sectional, like many of the others on this list, can be configured to be left-facing or right-facing depending on your living room setup, and we particularly like that in case you tend to switch up your design layout (we certainly do).

At just 62 inches wide, Sabai’s Essential Loveseat Sectional demonstrates how even the smallest living rooms can accommodate a proper chaise lounge. The compact design features clean lines, a generously sized chaise, and supportive foam cushions, offering the ideal spot to stretch out after a long day. Adding to the sectional’s appeal is its focus on sustainability. It combines a frame made from wood certified by the Forest Stewardship Council (FSC)—a third-party verification that ensures ethical wood sourcing—with upholstery fashioned from recycled natural materials. It’s also easy to clean and maintain, largely thanks to its washable slipcovers, sold separately—a welcome feature for anyone whose couch often doubles as a dining chair for meals eaten over the coffee table. Suffice it to say, this sectional is particularly well-suited to apartment living.

CB2’s Deseo sectional brings a little drama to small-space living, courtesy of rich, praline-brown performance velvet, broad arms, and a low-slung silhouette that practically begs you to sink in and stay awhile. The three-piece configuration, matching ottoman, and option for an extra-deep seat only extend the lounging possibilities and allow you to take advantage of every inch of your space. If brown doesn’t fit your space’s palette, worry not; the Deseo comes in plenty of other glamorous colors, including seven other performance velvet upholstery options.

Serious lounging is the goal for Sundays’ Movie Night sectional, with oversized cushions, deep seats, and a relaxed silhouette that makes it difficult to resist the urge to spend the afternoon totally horizontal. The cream linen upholstery adds to its laid-back feel, while the movable ottoman lets you stretch out or create a more open seating arrangement when company comes over. The company’s own LiveLife performance fabric is stain-resistant, moisture-repellent, and washable, with removable slipcovers that make living with a light-colored sofa significantly less intimidating.

Interior Define takes customization very seriously, and the Sloan sectional showcases how having such options can pay off in a small living room. Choose your preferred upholstery, cushion fill, seat depth, and leg style, then fine-tune the dimensions to suit your floor plan. The reversible chaise adds another layer of flexibility, allowing you to change its orientation, in case you rearrange your furniture or move to a new place with a different layout.

Contributor Malia Griggs tested the Sloan in her own 196-square-foot living room, where she found that its supportive cushions, tailored silhouette, and carefully selected dimensions worked beautifully alongside a narrow entryway. “If you enjoy tailoring your décor to your taste, are willing to pay a bit more for quality, and have the patience for a made-to-order process, this is a wonderful sectional—especially for a smaller space,” she added.

What Features Should I Look for in a Sectional for Small Living Rooms?

Modular furniture—any sectional that comes in several parts so you can adjust your sofa to suit the configurations—is built with flexibility in mind, making it the ideal contender for a small living space. We have tested and loved Castlery modular sofas, but a search for modular sectional should give you plenty of other options. A room tight on space can benefit from couches with storage, like those from Albany Park. Now you’ll be able to minimize the clutter and extra blankets by stowing them underneath your seat.

How Do I Arrange a Sectional in a Small Space?

Don’t fall for the tiniest sectional you can find, but rather invest in something well-made—it plays a big part in your living room, after all. While it may seem counterintuitive, Nashville interior designer Amanda Khouri says, “Filling a small room with small furniture can make it feel…well, smaller.”

Designer Alicia Murphy used a sectional in the living room of a teensy trailer she converted into a plush family getaway in Montauk, New York, for this very reason: “If you use a corner sectional rather than a sofa and chair, you most likely will add seating for two or three more people,” Murphy explains, noting that the alternative prevents you from having about 36 inches of dead space in the corner.”

Another way to make it work? Set an L-shaped sectional across from two club chairs to create a conversation zone, advises interior designer Suzanne Dawson.

What Do I Look for in a High-Quality Sectional?

When you’re working with a small space, quality counts. It’s much harder to get away with a mid-century modern knock-off when it’s one of four furniture pieces packed into the room. When shopping for a sectional, evaluate the materials and care instructions. Avoid anything with plywood, and polyester when you can. Seek out solid wood construction, such as walnut, oak, or teak; and premium fabrics, like linen, leather, or chenille. You’ll also want to keep the warranty and return policy in mind, in case you want to send back a piece that doesn’t work for you.

#5013. After a Year of Washes, These Towels Are as Plush and Absorbent as the Day I Got Them

Utah-based bedding, bath, and loungewear brand Cozy Earth has built a $100 million business around bamboo. The brand is perhaps most loved for its luxe sheets, but we’ve also tested (and generally loved) its pajamas, blankets, and cotton-and-bamboo towels. In fact, most of our shopping team owns at least one Cozy Earth product, regarding them as “ultrasoft pieces that will reign forever in our linen closets,” as senior shopping editor Nashia Baker puts it.

The brand’s bamboo sheets have taken best overall in both our bamboo and cooling sheets roundups. Its towels have earned their own spots in our guides on the most absorbent bath towels, the best quick-dry towels, and the best waffle towel sets. Contributor Megan Wahn was sold on the waffle bath sheets after dunking a corner in a bowl of water: “I could literally see as the parts of the towel that were not submerged soaked up the water,” she says.

Towel marketing tends to promise softness and absorbency in the same breath, but in my experience, it’s rare that both are true. Cozy Earth’s Luxe Bath Towels are the exception. After a full calendar year of countless showers, followed by washing in New York City laundromats, where the dryers have one setting (and it’s “inferno”) they are still exceptional towels.

The feel was memorable from the start: plush and not the least bit scratchy. At 600 grams per square meter (GSM), a unit of measure for a towel’s weight, it also has real heft. The towel’s blend of cotton and bamboo viscose, woven using what the brand calls zero-twist technology, leaves no fabric loops for your hand to catch on. But despite lacking the shag-carpet texture of some fluffy towels, this soft, smooth Cozy Earth model remains absorbent enough to dry you with ease.

A year later the most impressive quality of these towels is how little they’ve changed. They’ve been through more showers than I can count and a year of intense washes (the care label calls for tumble drying on low, an option my laundromat doesn’t offer), and they’re still as soft to the touch as when I first got them. Plenty of cotton towels demonstrate their best absorbency in their first months, and then never again. The Cozy Earth Luxe towels still dry me off as well as they did on day one, which is something I can’t say for most towels I’ve owned (and I’ve tested a lot of them). In my experience, plush towels go stiff and absorbent ones go thin, but these have done neither.

The one concession to the laundromat is color. My off-white pair of towels has picked up a faint blue-gray tint over the year, though they still pass for white. For this I blame the machines more than the towels, which deserve better care than I can give them….

At $118 (currently $88.50 for Black Friday), I won’t pretend the Luxe Bath Towels set isn’t an investment. You can buy a 12-pack for the same price on Amazon. But these are my favorite bath towels due to their durability as much as their feel—a luxe combination that’s hard to replicate on a tight budget. Towels that stay soft and absorbent for a month are easy to find; it’s much rarer for options to hold their quality for a year and beyond. If you’re replacing a full bath-towel set, I recommend starting here instead of cycling through cheaper towels that end up costing you the same once you’ve replaced them a few times.

To outfit the whole bathroom, Cozy Earth sells the Complete Luxe Bath Bundle. The hand towels and washcloths are just as good as the towels.

#5008. 16 Best Coffee Tables for Every Style and Budget (2026)

With their placement at the center of the living room, the best coffee tables can really unify the space—coming in every shape, size, and material you can think of. The hard part is browsing through pages and pages of options online to find the one that fits the vibe and style of your space—from smaller end table designs and nesting coffee tables to options made of reclaimed wood.

In this buying guide, you’ll find a curated selection of picks including some that I can personally vouch for—the Castlery Harper Lift-Top Coffee Table and Ikea Stockholm Coffee Table, specifically—along with other great options that score high marks from on-staff testers and contributors for their design and style. Tested picks include info on what makes each coffee table stand out, how each person uses it in their space, and anything else buyers should keep in mind. So no matter your aesthetic preference—from sleek and Scandinavian to midcentury modern—there’s a piece just begging to be given pride of place in your home.

“While the most attractive furniture pieces aren’t always the most practical, Castlery’s Harper lift-top coffee table looks elevated and offers ample storage. It’s so easy to hide away items like spare throw blankets or extra cushions in the hidden compartment or even use the uplifted surface as a desk (or dining table). It’s got a pretty universal design that would work with most spaces like your living room or even a home office, and I’m a big fan of the rounded edges that feel more gentle to bump into than angular tables. One thing I’ll note is the piece is relatively heavy (about 125 pounds) and has no legs, so it might not be the easiest piece to move by yourself.” —Audrey Lee, shopping editor

“We first purchased this table in 2022 and own both the 38- and 28-inch versions. At the time, I was looking for a coffee table that checked three boxes: solid wood construction; minimal, clean-lined design; and something relatively child-friendly. This met all those criteria and then some. Our son climbed on it, colored on it, and ran his cars and trucks over it daily, and it still looks great. Earlier this year, we purchased the second table for a different space. When it arrived, it was a bit high for our couch, so we had our building’s brilliant superintendent help us take a couple of inches off the legs. I don’t think a modification like that could successfully be done with a table of poor quality—this one survived the process beautifully and no one would ever be able to tell the difference.” —Shoko Wanger, AD contributor

No specific instructions, though it is treated with a waterborne lacquer surface treatment

This table is very lightweight and not the most stable. I inherited it a while back from a friend of mine with an active toddler who was worried about it tipping over. Having experienced a quick tilt or two in my child-free household, I think my friend’s instincts were spot on. This table looks great, but it is certainly not the steadiest option around.” —Rachel Fletcher, AD contributor

This Ikea table is a true budget-friendly option that has almost 300 five-star reviews to its credit. It has a rust-resistant galvanized steel body, with a streamlined mesh top that works even outdoors since water passes through it like a sieve. Some also noted that inventory tends to go in and out of stock (especially in the summer months), so we’d recommend ordering now while it’s ready to ship. warranty, too.

The Shou Sugi Ban coffee table from House of Leon is our pick if you’re aiming for an austere, wabi-sabi aesthetic. Unlike other mass-produced styles, the beauty of this piece lies in the ancient Japanese technique of charring wood, a process which reveals a refined, hardened finish to the grain after successive rounds of burning. Each piece possesses a distinct finish and asymmetry, so no two are exactly alike.

In the category of discreet storage, Article’s Karl table brings its A game. The lift-top mechanism brings versatility and hidden storage (just think of all the scented candles that will finally have a place to call home), while the streamlined design is easy to incorporate into any space, whether you’re looking to accentuate a coastal theme or collecting more Japandi-leaning decor this year. A versatile range of finishes—white oak, medium smoked oak, and a deep walnut hue—also ensures your pick aligns with your wood preferences.

For the burl-skeptics, this round top-pick from AD100 honoree firm Pierce & Ward feels like a conscious toe-dip rather than a radicalized nose dive. The inlay detail offers just a hint of texture, while the surrounding oak veneer and modern, rounded legs distinctly appeal to the more minimally-minded design connoisseurs among us.

Anointing your living room with a Noguchi coffee table won’t go unnoticed by serious designheads: It’s a piece that underscores any collector’s proclivity for modern design. The sculptural legs are the star of the show, but don’t underestimate the power of a good area rug—a glass coffee table serves as a nice visual anchor to a graphic design underfoot.

The softened edges of this bronzed table create a silhouette we’d describe as delicately industrial. A handsome leather sofa might help play up the textural elements of the distressed finish, or you could lean into contrasts with a clean-looking beige or tasteful performance linen.

Following the rule of thumb for all furniture, you’ll want at least a foot of breathing room around your coffee table to keep a comfortable walkway. Most retailers, like Castelry, recommend a two-thirds approach to measuring—meaning that your coffee table should be no longer than two-thirds the length of your sofa. While the standard height for coffee tables is between 16 and 18 inches, if your sofa is lower (like a classic Togo) or taller, you may want to adjust for a more comfortable foot rest and arm reach.

From wood and metal to stone and acrylic, there are plenty of material options for you to navigate on your buying journey. Stone coffee tables are ideal for those looking to make a design statement. Just consider the drama that an intricately veined marble surface can add to a moody and modern living room, like in Harris Reed’s London home. A wood coffee table is the most design agnostic: Choose a rich wood tone to lean more mid-century or a natural oak for a minimalist, Scandi feel. Wood coffee tables are known to show water stains and scuffs more than glass or stone, so ensuring you have the right cleaners is key to longevity. In small spaces, a glass or acrylic coffee table can provide an optical illusion, taking up minimal visual real estate while still giving you a surface to set down a drink or burn your favorite candle.

#5003. 11 Best Dining Tables to Elevate Take-Out Nights (2026)

Even if you order takeout (almost) every evening, the best dining tables can make all of the difference for elevating your dishes, down to the cold leftovers you’ve reheated for dinner. You don’t have to be a regular hostess to know that these furniture pieces are more than a place to eat; they double as a surface to display your latest tableware finds, function as a desk, and even help facilitate conversation. Whether you’re looking to outfit a quaint breakfast nook or source something with panache for a formal dining area, the best dining tables bring in just the right amount of personality without distracting from what’s actually important—the people you’re eating with.

Ahead, we’re highlighting our 11 favorites, from a perfectly mod pedestal to an extendable farmhouse beauty. Looking for a dining table that was made for small spaces? We have options for you too.

Paired with the Meja dining chairs, Article’s Plumas table helped senior shopping editor Nashia Baker convert an empty living room nook into a dining setup. #5000. The 7 Best Facial Hair Removal Tools, Tested by Editors

Walk don’t run to Amazon because our best overall recommendation is 26% off RN.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

There are tons of different facial hair removal methods for women, including laser hair removal, waxing, and epilators. I have tried them all, including electric razors and dermaplaning tools. As WH’s beauty editor, I've sugared my face and body and interviewed dozens of derms about preventing dreaded razor bumps and unsightly ingrown hairs. Some facial hair removal methods take only a few minutes, while others can be pretty painful but require minimal upkeep. Finding the right method or combination of methods that work for your needs might take a few tries, but I've got you covered with my beauty editor-vetted recommendations. These editor-tested and dermatologist-recommended suggestions start at just $10.

Yes, this is the priciest recommendation, but it's totally worth the splurge. Braun works in just one treatment session and boasts smart features that justify the added cost and our top spot. The results are immediate, long-lasting, and include three attachments for the face, bikini line, and rest of the body.

Skin i·expert is Braun’s only laser made with a proprietary SensoAdapt technology that scans your skin tone and automatically adjusts the light intensity for an effective and safer hair removal experience. The software is controlled with the Braun smartphone app. It also sends alerts when you missed a spot, used the wrong device head, applied too much pressure, or used it too quickly.

Wohlner relies on this device every few weeks to remove her self-described "super coarse hair." Since laser hair removal isn't permanent, some hairs will still pop up. However, they grow back slower, which is a major win for Wohlner.

Laser hair removal also gets a pretty bad rep for being painful. While the Braun isn't equipped with any cooling sensation to relieve discomfort, the pain is relatively mild. "I felt a little zippiness here and there, but nothing I could actually describe as painful—more like a super quick, tiny pinch," says Wohlner.

As for the results, they were solid. Wohlner didn't have to shave her hair for weeks after the first treatment, and three sessions later, her hair regrowth slowed down. The upfront cost is pricey, but it'll save you from purchasing expensive pro-grade appointments and allow you to remove hair whenever and wherever you choose.

Finishing Touch is an underrated brand. I previously included the brand’s electric shaver with a built-in LED ring light to illuminate hairs I wouldn't usually see in my bathroom. However, it’s no longer being sold and cost more than double the price. This slimmer version is the ideal facial hair removal tool for budget-conscious shoppers, and I wish I ordered mine sooner.

I can achieve similar results using razors three times the price. The Flawless device is also so much prettier than boring razors. The rose gold hair remover is rather discreet and looks similar to dermaplaning tool. It features a small head that contours along the curves of my face, while the blade ensures I have the smoothest trim.

This device works great if you have sensitive skin that's easily irritated. It's gentle enough to use daily, and I love keeping mine on hand at the office to check my face before walking into a meeting.

The only caveat is Flawless is a razor, so it cuts hair at the top layer of skin, meaning hair will grow back relatively fast. So, as long as you don't mind regular trims, this is an epic choice.

After two years of on-and-off testing, Flamingo still makes my favorite razor. Its sharp blades that don’t tug or pull at my sensitive skin elevate Flamingo to the top of my shower routine.

The brand suggests replacing the blades every four to six weeks, but each blade lasts me closer to eight weeks, meaning a regular four-pack of blades lasts me approximately 32 weeks (eight months) before becoming dull. Plus, this razor set is comfy to use while shaving down to my ankles. It curves along my kneecaps and pits with ease, and it hasn’t caused any nicks or ingrown hairs along my bikini line.

I’ll continue using Flamingo, and at just $16, you can also enjoy smooth skin which includes the razor, four blades, and a holder that fits neatly in cramped spaces like my New York City shower.

While I don't mind using a traditional manual razor or dermaplaning, it's worth spending a little extra on this electric device, says Wohlner. This Philips electric razor has a large head and self-sharpening blade to rapidly remove facial hair. Wohlner's been using this facial hair removal tool for hairs and considers it the most effective method for eliminating stubborn upper lip and chin hairs. "While it's too big to remove hair on my brows, it's awesome for those larger areas of the face when you're short on time and want to look smooth and stubble-free," she says.

Many other tools can irritate, especially around the upper lip area, which occasionally turns into bumps. This shaver has a hypoallergenic stainless steel head that doesn’t irritate sensitive skin, quickly removing unwanted hair in a single pass without causing pain or discomfort. This device keeps Wohlner's face smooth, and it always glides over the skin effortlessly without leaving it red and raw. It even comes with a built-in mirror in the cap to touch-up on the go.

Pro tip: The large shaver head does tend to get clogged after a few uses, which can be prevented if you wipe it down with alcohol after every use. Taking care of your device will ensure you get the closest shave and that it lasts for years.

The idea of gliding a sharp razor across my face was initially intimidating, but I haven't scratched myself since using the Dermaflash. Unlike cheaper options with dull blades, the Dermaflash provides the most professional-grade shave I've ever experienced while testing dozens of dermaplaning tools. The sharp blade features proprietary Microfine Edges, which are tiny corrugations that get super close to my face without any discomfort.

Most dermaplaning tools only have simple blades, but Dermaflash features a gentle pulsing technology that further removes peach fuzz and dead skin cells instantly and safely, making it handy for beginners and seasoned dermaplaning users alike. My results rival pro treatments without the hassle of going to a spa and without the excessive upkeep cost. This tool easily glides across my face, which would normally be a challenge with cheaper, manual dermaplaning tools. Since using this device, I noticed my face cream sinks in quickly, and my foundation looks even more natural.

I try to use this tool every two weeks to maintain my smooth results, but TBH, I use it monthly, which is better for my wallet. The brand advises swapping out the blade after every use for the smoothest results, so anything to stretch my beauty budget is always a win. FWIW: Even a monthly treatment prevents my makeup from pilling, so this tool will remain a consistent part of my routine.

Sugaring is epic because the paste only sticks to the hair, not the skin. It's far less painful than waxing and easier to apply.

I've previously only done professional sugaring, but the at-home process is the same. After heating the roller in the microwave, I apply the stick directly to the area I want sugared, lay down a strip, and pull it off in the opposite direction of the hair growth. The fabric strips are a little big to use on my face, especially on the chin or upper lip, but I can cut them in half, and they work perfectly.

Most sugaring wax is packaged in a tub that can tip over and cause a huge, sticky mess. Schick comes in a deodorant-like stick that easily rolls onto my skin. I like to warm the stick up in the microwave in 5-second increments until the paste has a honey-like consistency. I also need a light layer of sugaring wax to achieve the same results as other alternatives, meaning my roller can last for a few months.

I initially missed a few hairs, so I had to follow up with a fresh swipe of sugaring paste. With some practice, my technique improved over time, and soon enough, it only took me a few minutes to wax the lower half of my face. At just $13 a wand, this at-home technique is much cheaper than going to a salon where I'd easily pay double the price, and for that alone, I'll continue using this kit.

This hair removal cream isn't the Nair you grew up on. It's a prescription-strength cream specifically formulated to slow hair growth on the face. The key ingredients include eflornithine (6%), which blocks the enzyme ornithine decarboxylase (ODC) that stimulates hair growth. Each batch is compounded together shortly before shipping, ensuring maximum potency.

I've tried many hair removal creams, which sometimes cause redness and irritation, so I was worried Musely would be a problem. Musely provides instant hair-free results, and I noticed my regrowth is slower and my skin remains smoother with regular use, compared to other creams I’ve tried. It has a light tingling sensation, letting me know the cream is working. Unlike cheaper drugstore alternatives, Musely hasn't irritated my skin. It also doesn't have a super aggressive chemical smell like other alternatives I tested, and that alone is a win in my book.

The major downside of hair removal creams, including Musely, is that I have to adjust when I use my tretinoin prescription around hair removal days. After Musely hair removal, I typically wait a few days before safely applying my tretinoin cream. Otherwise, I might experience redness, irritation, and over-exfoliate my skin. Musely is a worthwhile product, but I prefer dermaplaning or shaving because I value acne-free skin over a fast-acting, pain-free hair removal process.

While laser hair removal isn't exactly permanent, it can effectively slow hair growth. While the upfront cost is a tad of a jumpscare, these devices typically contain about 200,000 flashes, which is equivalent to 780 at-home treatments, says Dr. Nazarian.

These are tried-and-true hair removal tools that are cheap, readily accessible, and provide instant results. Since dermaplaning cuts the hairs at the top layer of skin, they'll grow back quicker than laser hair removal or waxing, says Dr. Murphy-Rose. So be ready to use this technique 1-4 times monthly, depending on how fast your hair regrows.

Dermaplaning tools and razors are either manual or electric powered by batteries or a USB or USB-C cable.

Ultimately, opt for the electric devices if you want a fast and powerful tool that lasts for years and don't mind the upfront cost. Choose a manual tool if you would rather save the cash for other beauty products, like an LED face mask or a red light therapy device for hair growth.
